<table class="disp-formula p" id="Equ4"><tr><td class="formula"><math id="d33e2216" display="block"><mi mathvariant="bold">AL</mi><mspace width="0.25em"></mspace><mrow><mo>(</mo><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>X</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>p</mi></mrow></msub><mo>,</mo><msup><mrow><mi>X</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>′</mo></mrow></msup><mo>,</mo><mi>d</mi></mrow><mo>)</mo></mrow><mo>=</mo><msub><mrow><mi>w</mi></mrow><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">μ</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi></mrow></msub><mo>,</mo><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">σ</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>d</mi></mrow></msub></mrow></msub><mrow><mo>[</mo><mrow><mi>d</mi></mrow><mo>]</mo></mrow><mo>⋅</mo><mfenced close=")" open="("><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>F</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">Δ</mi></mrow></msub><mfenced close=")" open="("><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>X</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>p</mi></mrow></msub></mrow></mfenced><mo>⋆</mo><msub><mrow><mi>F</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">Δ</mi></mrow></msub><mrow><mo>(</mo><mrow><msup><mrow><mi>X</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>′</mo></mrow></msup></mrow><mo>)</mo></mrow><mrow><mo>[</mo><mrow><mi>d</mi></mrow><mo>]</mo></mrow></mrow></mfenced><mo>,</mo></math></td></tr></table>
<table class="disp-formula p" id="Equ5"><tr><td class="formula"><math id="d33e2303" display="block"><mrow><mo>(</mo><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>F</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">Δ</mi></mrow></msub><mfenced close=")" open="("><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>X</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>p</mi></mrow></msub></mrow></mfenced><mo>⋆</mo><msub><mrow><mi>F</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">Δ</mi></mrow></msub><mfenced close=")" open="("><mrow><msup><mrow><mi>X</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>′</mo></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mo>)</mo></mrow><mrow><mo>[</mo><mrow><mi>d</mi></mrow><mo>]</mo></mrow><mo>=</mo><msubsup><mrow><mo mathsize="big">∑</mo></mrow><mrow><mi>n</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>M</mi></mrow></msubsup><msub><mrow><mi>F</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">Δ</mi></mrow></msub><mfenced close=")" open="("><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>X</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>p</mi></mrow></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mo>[</mo><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>r</mi><mi>t</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>min</mi></mrow></msub><mo>+</mo><mi>n</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">Δ</mi></mrow><mo>]</mo></mrow><mo>⋅</mo><msub><mrow><mi>F</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">Δ</mi></mrow></msub><mfenced close=")" open="("><mrow><msup><mrow><mi>X</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>′</mo></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mo>[</mo><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>r</mi><mi>t</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>min</mi></mrow></msub><mo>+</mo><mi>n</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">Δ</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>d</mi></mrow><mo>]</mo></mrow></math></td></tr></table>
<p>Where <em>d</em> is the <em>rt</em>-displacement between <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" id="IEq46"><math id="d33e2418"><msub><mrow><mi>X</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>p</mi></mrow></msub></math></span> and <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" id="IEq47"><math id="d33e2429"><mi>X</mi><mo>′</mo></math></span>, <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" id="IEq48"><math id="d33e2437"><msub><mrow><mi>r</mi><mi>t</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>min</mi></mrow></msub></math></span> is minimum <em>rt</em> of the reference bounding box, M is the length of <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" id="IEq49"><math id="d33e2452"><msub><mrow><mi>F</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">Δ</mi></mrow></msub><mrow><mo>(</mo><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>X</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>p</mi></mrow></msub></mrow><mo>)</mo></mrow><mo>,</mo></math></span> and <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" id="IEq50"><math id="d33e2475"><msub><mrow><mi>w</mi></mrow><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">μ</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi></mrow></msub><mo>,</mo><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">σ</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>d</mi></mrow></msub></mrow></msub><mrow><mo>[</mo><mrow><mi>d</mi></mrow><mo>]</mo></mrow></math></span> is the expected drift function. Initially, the expected drift function <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" id="IEq51"><math id="d33e2504"><msub><mrow><mi>w</mi></mrow><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">μ</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi></mrow></msub><mo>,</mo><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">σ</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>d</mi></mrow></msub></mrow></msub><mrow><mo>[</mo><mrow><mi>d</mi></mrow><mo>]</mo></mrow></math></span> is 1 for all values of <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" id="IEq52"><math id="d33e2533"><mi>d</mi></math></span>. Later, the expected drift function is determined by the <em>rt</em>-drift map (Section C.2) using the expected <em>rt</em>-drift (<span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" id="IEq53"><math id="d33e2546"><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">μ</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi></mrow></msub></math></span>) and the <em>rt</em>-drift uncertainty (<span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" id="IEq54"><math id="d33e2563"><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">σ</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>d</mi></mrow></msub></math></span>) between samples k to k’. With <em>rt</em>-drift map information, <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" id="IEq55"><math id="d33e2578"><msub><mrow><mi>w</mi></mrow><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">μ</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi></mrow></msub><mo>,</mo><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">σ</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>d</mi></mrow></msub></mrow></msub><mrow><mo>[</mo><mrow><mi>d</mi></mrow><mo>]</mo></mrow></math></span> is 1 for <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" id="IEq56"><math id="d33e2607"><mi>d</mi></math></span> in the 2<span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" id="IEq57"><math id="d33e2614"><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">σ</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>d</mi></mrow></msub></math></span> neighborhood of <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" id="IEq58"><math id="d33e2627"><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">μ</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi></mrow></msub></math></span>, and decays to 0 for <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" id="IEq59"><math id="d33e2640"><mi>d</mi></math></span> beyond this neighborhood.</p>
<p id="Par38">The limits of the candidate patch <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" id="IEq60"><math id="d33e2649"><msub><mrow><mi>X</mi><mo>′</mo></mrow><mrow><mi>p</mi></mrow></msub></math></span> are defined as <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" id="IEq61"><math id="d33e2661"><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>r</mi><mi>t</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>min</mi></mrow></msub><mo>+</mo><mspace width="0.25em"></mspace><msup><mrow><mi>d</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>*</mo></mrow></msup><mo>,</mo><mspace width="0.25em"></mspace><msub><mrow><mi>r</mi><mi>t</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>max</mi></mrow></msub><mspace width="0.25em"></mspace><mo>+</mo><mspace width="0.25em"></mspace><msup><mrow><mi>d</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>*</mo></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced><mo>×</mo><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>m</mi><mi>z</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>min</mi></mrow></msub><mo>−</mo><mi>m</mi><msub><mrow><mi>z</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>t</mi><mi>o</mi><mi>l</mi></mrow></msub><mo>,</mo><mspace width="0.25em"></mspace><msub><mrow><mi>m</mi><mi>z</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>max</mi></mrow></msub><mo>+</mo><mi>m</mi><msub><mrow><mi>z</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>t</mi><mi>o</mi><mi>l</mi></mrow></msub></mrow></mfenced></math></span>, where <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" id="IEq62"><math id="d33e2733"><msup><mrow><mi>d</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>*</mo></mrow></msup></math></span> is the <em>rt</em>-displacement that maximized <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" id="IEq63"><math id="d33e2747"><mi>A</mi><mi>L</mi><mrow><mo>(</mo><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>X</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>p</mi></mrow></msub><mo>,</mo><mi>X</mi><mo>′</mo><mo>,</mo><mi>d</mi></mrow><mo>)</mo></mrow></math></span>. The candidate patch and the reference patch form a patch-group pair <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" id="IEq64"><math id="d33e2770"><mrow><mo>{</mo><mrow><msubsup><mrow><mi>X</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>p</mi></mrow><mrow><mfenced close=")" open="("><mrow><mi>k</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow></msubsup><mo>,</mo><msubsup><mrow><mi>X</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>p</mi></mrow><mrow><mfenced close=")" open="("><mrow><mi>k</mi><mo>′</mo></mrow></mfenced></mrow></msubsup></mrow><mo>}</mo></mrow></math></span>. After alignment of the patches, we calculate the cross-correlation score, which is the zero-mean normalized cross-correlation, to return values between -1 and 1:</p>
<table class="disp-formula p" id="Equ6"><tr><td class="formula"><math id="d33e2800" display="block"><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="bold-italic">XC</mi></mrow><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>X</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>p</mi></mrow></msub></mrow></msub><mrow><mo>(</mo><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>X</mi><mo>′</mo></mrow><mrow><mi>p</mi></mrow></msub></mrow><mo>)</mo></mrow><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mi>M</mi></mrow></mfrac><msubsup><mrow><mo mathsize="big">∑</mo></mrow><mrow><mi>n</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mi>M</mi></mrow></msubsup><mfenced close=")" open="("><mrow><mfrac><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>F</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">Δ</mi></mrow></msub><mfenced close=")" open="("><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>X</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>p</mi></mrow></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mo>[</mo><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>r</mi><mi>t</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>min</mi></mrow></msub><mo>+</mo><mi>n</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">Δ</mi></mrow><mo>]</mo></mrow><mspace width="0.25em"></mspace><mo>−</mo><mspace width="0.25em"></mspace><msub><mrow><mi>μ</mi></mrow><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>X</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>p</mi></mrow></msub></mrow></msub></mrow><mrow><msqrt><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>σ</mi></mrow><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>X</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>p</mi></mrow></msub></mrow></msub></mrow></msqrt></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced><mspace width="1.0em"></mspace><mfenced close=")" open="("><mrow><mfrac><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>F</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">Δ</mi></mrow></msub><mfenced close=")" open="("><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>X</mi><mo>′</mo></mrow><mrow><mi>p</mi></mrow></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mo>[</mo><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>r</mi><mi>t</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>min</mi></mrow></msub><mo>+</mo><mi>n</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">Δ</mi><mo>+</mo><msup><mrow><mi>d</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>*</mo></mrow></msup></mrow><mo>]</mo></mrow><mspace width="0.25em"></mspace><mo>−</mo><mspace width="0.25em"></mspace><msub><mrow><mi>μ</mi></mrow><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>X</mi><mo>′</mo></mrow><mrow><mi>p</mi></mrow></msub></mrow></msub></mrow><mrow><msqrt><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>σ</mi></mrow><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>X</mi><mo>′</mo></mrow><mrow><mi>p</mi></mrow></msub></mrow></msub></mrow></msqrt></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></math></td></tr></table>
<p>where <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" id="IEq65"><math id="d33e2962"><msub><mrow><mi>μ</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>X</mi></mrow></msub></math></span> and <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" id="IEq66"><math id="d33e2973"><msub><mrow><mi>σ</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>X</mi></mrow></msub></math></span> are the mean and standard deviation of <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" id="IEq67"><math id="d33e2984"><msub><mrow><mi>F</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">Δ</mi></mrow></msub><mfenced close=")" open="("><mrow><mi>X</mi></mrow></mfenced></math></span> for patch <em>X</em>, and M is the length of <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" id="IEq68"><math id="d33e3003"><msub><mrow><mi>F</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">Δ</mi></mrow></msub><mrow><mo>(</mo><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>X</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>p</mi></mrow></msub></mrow><mo>)</mo></mrow></math></span>. Patch pairs above the cross-correlation threshold are kept as correlated patches. We seed with <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" id="IEq69"><math id="d33e3024"><msubsup><mrow><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>c</mi><mi>c</mi></mrow><mrow><mrow><mo>(</mo><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow><mo>)</mo></mrow></mrow></msubsup><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></span>. After Stage-1, the cross-correlation cutoff is set to the 5th percentile of per-peak cross-correlation among pins and is recomputed once after Stage-2; these learned cutoffs replace the seeds for all subsequent filtering.</p>
<p id="Par39">After we obtain pairs of cross-correlated patches, all pairs that share the same referential patch are combined into a cohort wide patch-group <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" id="IEq70"><math id="d33e3046"><msub><mrow><mrow><mo>{</mo><mrow><msubsup><mrow><mi>X</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>p</mi></mrow><mrow><mfenced close=")" open="("><mrow><mi>k</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow></msubsup></mrow><mo>}</mo></mrow></mrow><mrow><mi>k</mi><mo>∈</mo><msub><mrow><mi>N</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>p</mi></mrow></msub></mrow></msub></math></span>, where <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" id="IEq71"><math id="d33e3075"><msub><mrow><mi>N</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>p</mi></mrow></msub></math></span> is the number of patch pairs.</p></section><section id="Sec17"><h3 class="pmc_sec_title">C. Estimating <em>rt</em> Drift</h3>
<p id="Par40">To estimate the <em>rt</em> drift between pairs of correlated patched, we define “pins” as a subset of the correlated patches found in most sample files with above-average autocorrelation and cross-correlation scores. Pins are updated at each stage of mzLearn (via updated filtering parameters and drift information) and are used for the next stage of the peak improvement process.</p>
<section id="Sec18"><h4 class="pmc_sec_title">Stage-1 Pins and rt-Drift Map</h4>
<p id="Par41">The stage-1 pins are patches that cross-correlate across all samples (no missing values) with initial group-average cross-correlation scores exceeding 0.8 and are used to obtain <em>rt</em>-drift map information. Stage 1 pins, the set of T robust patches h<sub>1</sub>, …, h<sub>T</sub> spanning the entire cohort of files, are then used to generate non-linear drift maps between any desired pair of samples. Specifically, the drift <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" id="IEq72"><math id="d33e3108"><msub><mrow><mover accent="true"><mrow><mi>d</mi></mrow><mo>→</mo></mover></mrow><mrow><mi>k</mi><mo>→</mo><msup><mrow><mi>k</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>′</mo></mrow></msup></mrow></msub><mfenced close=")" open="("><mrow><mi>x</mi></mrow></mfenced></math></span> from any sample <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" id="IEq73"><math id="d33e3131"><mi>k</mi></math></span> to <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" id="IEq74"><math id="d33e3139"><msup><mrow><mi>k</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>′</mo></mrow></msup></math></span> at the desired coordinates <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" id="IEq75"><math id="d33e3150"><mi>x</mi><mo>=</mo><mfenced close=")" open="("><mrow><mi>r</mi><mi>t</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>m</mi><mo>/</mo><mi>z</mi></mrow></mfenced></math></span> can be interpolated by computing the weighted average (<span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" id="IEq76"><math id="d33e3166"><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">μ</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi></mrow></msub></math></span>) of the <em>rt</em>-drift of pins, where pins closer to the coordinate of interest are given a higher weight and pins further away are given a lower weight. The weighted standard deviation (<span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" id="IEq77"><math id="d33e3182"><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">σ</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>d</mi></mrow></msub></math></span>) of the <em>rt</em> drift is used to measure the error tolerances in the drift. The weighted 1<sup>st</sup> and 99<sup>th</sup> percentiles of the pin drifts are the lower (<span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" id="IEq78"><math id="d33e3202"><mi>l</mi><msub><mrow><mi>b</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>d</mi></mrow></msub></math></span>) and upper bounds (<span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" id="IEq79"><math id="d33e3214"><mi>u</mi><msub><mrow><mi>b</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>d</mi></mrow></msub></math></span>) of the drift at <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" id="IEq80"><math id="d33e3226"><mi>x</mi></math></span> in sample <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" id="IEq81"><math id="d33e3233"><mi>k</mi></math></span>, respectively.</p></section><section id="Sec19"><h4 class="pmc_sec_title">Re-alignment of patches via the rt-drift map</h4>
<p id="Par42">We repeat the cross-correlation primitive for every file pair in the data set using the inferred <em>rt</em>-drift map. Unlike the cross-correlation used to find stage-1 pins, the stage-1 drift map <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" id="IEq82"><math id="d33e3247"><msub><mrow><mover accent="true"><mrow><mi>d</mi></mrow><mo>→</mo></mover></mrow><mrow><mi>k</mi><mo>→</mo><msup><mrow><mi>k</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>′</mo></mrow></msup></mrow></msub><mfenced close=")" open="("><mrow><mi>x</mi></mrow></mfenced></math></span> is now available to guide the cross-correlation procedure. The relevant <em>rt</em>-drift information for the cross-correlation of patch p in file k to sample k’ is obtained by considering the stage-1 drift map at the peak position of the patch:</p>
<table class="disp-formula p" id="Equ7"><tr><td class="formula"><math id="d33e3273" display="block"><msub><mrow><mover accent="true"><mrow><mi>d</mi></mrow><mo>→</mo></mover></mrow><mrow><mi>k</mi><mo>→</mo><msup><mrow><mi>k</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>′</mo></mrow></msup></mrow></msub><mfenced close=")" open="("><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">Pk</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>r</mi><mi>t</mi></mrow></msub><mrow><mo>(</mo><mrow><msubsup><mrow><mi>X</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>p</mi></mrow><mrow><mfenced close=")" open="("><mrow><mi>k</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow></msubsup></mrow><mo>)</mo></mrow><mo>,</mo><mspace width="0.25em"></mspace><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">Pk</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>m</mi><mi>z</mi></mrow></msub><mrow><mo>(</mo><mrow><msubsup><mrow><mi>X</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>p</mi></mrow><mrow><mfenced close=")" open="("><mrow><mi>k</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow></msubsup></mrow><mo>)</mo></mrow></mrow></mfenced><mo>,</mo></math></td></tr></table>
<p>where <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" id="IEq83"><math id="d33e3338"><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">Pk</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>r</mi><mi>t</mi></mrow></msub><mrow><mo>(</mo><mrow><msubsup><mrow><mi>X</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>p</mi></mrow><mrow><mfenced close=")" open="("><mrow><mi>k</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow></msubsup></mrow><mo>)</mo></mrow></math></span> and <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" id="IEq84"><math id="d33e3365"><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">Pk</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>m</mi><mi>z</mi></mrow></msub><mrow><mo>(</mo><mrow><msubsup><mrow><mi>X</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>p</mi></mrow><mrow><mfenced close=")" open="("><mrow><mi>k</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow></msubsup></mrow><mo>)</mo></mrow></math></span> are the <em>rt</em> and <em>m/z</em> coordinates of the patch <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" id="IEq85"><math id="d33e3397"><msubsup><mrow><mspace width="0.25em"></mspace><mi>X</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>p</mi></mrow><mrow><mfenced close=")" open="("><mrow><mi>k</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow></msubsup></math></span> maximum intensity. The rt-drift information is then used to guide the alignment of <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" id="IEq86"><math id="d33e3413"><msubsup><mrow><mspace width="0.25em"></mspace><mi>X</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>p</mi></mrow><mrow><mfenced close=")" open="("><mrow><mi>k</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow></msubsup></math></span> with the corresponding signal in sample k’ via the cross-correlation primitive (Section B).</p></section></section><section id="Sec20"><h3 class="pmc_sec_title">D. Peak extraction via multi-step scissors</h3>
<p id="Par43">While most patch groups include data from one peak, some patch groups may contain multiple peaks with similar <em>m/z</em> and <em>rt</em> values, for example, peaks from isomeric metabolites. To separate multiple peaks in the same patch group, we apply a procedure named “scissors” according to the following steps:</p>
<ol class="list" style="list-style-type:lower-alpha">
<li><p id="Par44">Patch groups are split by gaps in <em>rt</em>. The reference sample (<span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" id="IEq87"><math id="d33e3448"><msub><mrow><mi>k</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></math></span>) adjusted <em>rt</em> data points <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" id="IEq88"><math id="d33e3462"><msubsup><mrow><mi>r</mi><mi>t</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>p</mi></mrow><mrow><mrow><mo>(</mo><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>k</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>i</mi></mrow></msub><mo>→</mo><msub><mrow><mi>k</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></mrow><mo>)</mo></mrow></mrow></msubsup></math></span> are collected from the individual patches in the patch group, <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" id="IEq89"><math id="d33e3490"><msub><mrow><mo>∪</mo></mrow><mrow><mi>i</mi></mrow></msub><msubsup><mrow><mi>r</mi><mi>t</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>p</mi></mrow><mrow><mrow><mo>(</mo><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>k</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>i</mi></mrow></msub><mo>→</mo><msub><mrow><mi>k</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></mrow><mo>)</mo></mrow></mrow></msubsup></math></span>. If there is a gap between consecutive <em>rt</em> values that is greater than <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" id="IEq90"><math id="d33e3527"><msub><mrow><mi>D</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>r</mi><mi>t</mi></mrow></msub></math></span>, the group is split at the gap.</p></li>
<li><p id="Par45">Patch groups are split by gaps in <em>m/z</em>. The reference sample (<span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" id="IEq91"><math id="d33e3547"><msub><mrow><mi>k</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></math></span>) adjusted <em>rt</em> and <em>m/z</em> data points <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" id="IEq92"><math id="d33e3564"><msubsup><mrow><mi>r</mi><mi>t</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>p</mi></mrow><mrow><mrow><mo>(</mo><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>k</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>i</mi></mrow></msub><mo>→</mo><msub><mrow><mi>k</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></mrow><mo>)</mo></mrow></mrow></msubsup><mo>,</mo><msubsup><mrow><mi>m</mi><mi>z</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>p</mi></mrow><mrow><mrow><mo>(</mo><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>k</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>i</mi></mrow></msub><mo>→</mo><msub><mrow><mi>k</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></mrow><mo>)</mo></mrow></mrow></msubsup></math></span> are collected from the individual patches in the patch group. If the group-wide <em>m/z</em>-width is greater than 10*mz<sub>tol</sub>(Da), the patch group is split by k-means clustering. The number of clusters for k-means clustering is chosen using DBSCAN, which maximizes the density of points within each cluster while minimizing the density between clusters<sup><a href="#CR72" class="usa-link" aria-describedby="CR72">72</a></sup>. New groups are created from each cluster of data points.</p></li>
<li><p id="Par46">Patch-groups are split at the <em>rt</em> position of the local intensity minima. mzLearn searches for the <em>rt</em>-locations of the intensity minima, <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" id="IEq93"><math id="d33e3636"><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">Min</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>r</mi><mi>t</mi></mrow></msub></math></span>, in a patch group, <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" id="IEq94"><math id="d33e3649"><msub><mrow><mrow><mo>{</mo><mrow><msubsup><mrow><mi>X</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>p</mi></mrow><mrow><mfenced close=")" open="("><mrow><mi>k</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow></msubsup></mrow><mo>}</mo></mrow></mrow><mrow><mi>k</mi><mo>∈</mo><msub><mrow><mi>N</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>p</mi></mrow></msub></mrow></msub></math></span>, with two strategies: In the first strategy, the intensity minima are measured from the combined patch group signal, <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" id="IEq95"><math id="d33e3678"><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">Min</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>r</mi><mi>t</mi></mrow></msub><mrow><mo>[</mo><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>F</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">Δ</mi></mrow></msub><mrow><mo>(</mo><mrow><msub><mrow><mo>∪</mo></mrow><mrow><mi>i</mi></mrow></msub><msubsup><mrow><mi>X</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>p</mi></mrow><mrow><mrow><mo>(</mo><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>k</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>i</mi></mrow></msub><mo>→</mo><msub><mrow><mi>k</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></mrow><mo>)</mo></mrow></mrow></msubsup></mrow><mo>)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo>]</mo></mrow></math></span>. In the second strategy, the intensity minima are determined by consensus: Intensity minima are found in each individual patch, and those locations are averaged, <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" id="IEq96"><math id="d33e3732"><mi mathvariant="normal">Avg</mi><mrow><mo>(</mo><mrow><msub><mrow><mrow><mo>{</mo><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">Min</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>r</mi><mi>t</mi></mrow></msub><mrow><mo>[</mo><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>F</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">Δ</mi></mrow></msub><mrow><mo>(</mo><mrow><msubsup><mrow><mi>X</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>p</mi></mrow><mrow><mrow><mo>(</mo><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>k</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>i</mi></mrow></msub><mo>→</mo><msub><mrow><mi>k</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></mrow><mo>)</mo></mrow></mrow></msubsup></mrow><mo>)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo>]</mo></mrow></mrow><mo>}</mo></mrow></mrow><mrow><mi>k</mi><mo>∈</mo><msub><mrow><mi>N</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>p</mi></mrow></msub></mrow></msub></mrow><mo>)</mo></mrow></math></span>.</p></li>
</ol>
<p id="Par47">The result of the multi-step scissor procedure is an expanded set of candidate peak-groups.</p></section><section id="Sec21"><h3 class="pmc_sec_title">E. Improving and refining peak groups</h3>
<p id="Par48">Peak groups are further filtered to remove noisy and mis-aligned peaks, based on the improved parameters learned from Stage-2 pins, which are a subset of candidate peak groups with high quality. Here, we consider the candidate peaks that are found in N<sub>tot</sub><span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" id="IEq97"><math id="d33e3807"><mfenced close=")" open="("><mrow><mn>0.8</mn><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>0.2</mn></mrow><mrow><mi>log</mi><mrow><mo>(</mo><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>N</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>t</mi><mi>o</mi><mi>t</mi></mrow></msub></mrow><mo>)</mo></mrow></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></math></span> samples, where N<sub>tot</sub> is the total number of samples. Features that have above-average autocorrelation scores and above-average cross-correlation scores are selected as stage-2 pins. Stage-2 pins are used to obtain a more accurate stage-2 drift map and to calculate quality thresholds.</p></section><section id="Sec22"><h3 class="pmc_sec_title">Alignment reliability score</h3>
<p id="Par49">The alignment reliability score, <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" id="IEq98"><math id="d33e3839"><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="bold-italic">AR</mi></mrow><mrow><mrow><mo>{</mo><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>X</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>p</mi></mrow></msub></mrow><mo>}</mo></mrow></mrow></msub><mrow><mo>(</mo><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>X</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>p</mi></mrow></msub></mrow><mo>)</mo></mrow></math></span> of an individual peak <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" id="IEq99"><math id="d33e3868"><msub><mrow><mi>X</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>p</mi></mrow></msub></math></span>, with respect to its peak group, <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" id="IEq100"><math id="d33e3879"><mrow><mo>{</mo><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>X</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>p</mi></mrow></msub></mrow><mo>}</mo></mrow></math></span>, as a measure of the consistency of the peak <em>rt</em>-location across all samples in the peak group. The adjusted peak coordinate, <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" id="IEq101"><math id="d33e3897"><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="bold">Pk</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="bold-italic">rt</mi></mrow></msub><mrow><mo>(</mo><mrow><msubsup><mrow><mi>X</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>p</mi></mrow><mrow><mrow><mo>(</mo><mrow><mi>k</mi><mo>→</mo><msub><mrow><mi>k</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></mrow><mo>)</mo></mrow></mrow></msubsup></mrow><mo>)</mo></mrow></math></span>, is the <em>rt</em>-coordinate of the k<sup>th</sup> sample’s maximum intensity value in the reference (sample <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" id="IEq102"><math id="d33e3937"><msub><mrow><mi>k</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></math></span>) coordinate system. The alignment reliability score compares this adjusted peak coordinate to the rest of the patches in the group to identify any outliers:</p>
<table class="disp-formula p" id="Equ8"><tr><td class="formula"><math id="d33e3948" display="block"><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="bold-italic">AR</mi></mrow><mrow><mfenced close="}" open="{"><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>X</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>p</mi></mrow></msub></mrow></mfenced></mrow></msub><mfenced close=")" open="("><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>X</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>p</mi></mrow></msub></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><msup><mrow><mi>e</mi></mrow><mrow><msup><mrow><mrow><mo>[</mo><mrow><mo>−</mo><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="bold">Pk</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="bold-italic">rt</mi></mrow></msub><mrow><mo>(</mo><mrow><msubsup><mrow><mi>X</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>p</mi></mrow><mrow><mrow><mo>(</mo><mrow><mi>k</mi><mo>→</mo><msub><mrow><mi>k</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></mrow><mo>)</mo></mrow></mrow></msubsup></mrow><mo>)</mo></mrow><mspace width="0.25em"></mspace><mo>−</mo><msub><mrow><mi>μ</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>r</mi></mrow></msub></mrow><mo>]</mo></mrow></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></msup><mo>/</mo><mn>2</mn><msup><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>σ</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>r</mi></mrow></msub></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math></td></tr></table>
<p>where <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" id="IEq103"><math id="d33e4033"><msub><mrow><mi>μ</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>r</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">Avg</mi><mrow><mo>(</mo><mrow><msub><mrow><mrow><mo>{</mo><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="bold">Pk</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="bold-italic">rt</mi></mrow></msub><mrow><mo>(</mo><mrow><msubsup><mrow><mi>X</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>p</mi></mrow><mrow><mrow><mo>(</mo><mrow><mi>k</mi><mo>→</mo><msub><mrow><mi>k</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></mrow><mo>)</mo></mrow></mrow></msubsup></mrow><mo>)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo>}</mo></mrow></mrow><mrow><mi>k</mi><mo>∈</mo><msub><mrow><mi>N</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>p</mi></mrow></msub></mrow></msub></mrow><mo>)</mo></mrow></math></span> and <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" id="IEq104"><math id="d33e4093"><msub><mrow><mi>σ</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>r</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mi>M</mi><mi>a</mi><mi>x</mi><mrow><mo>(</mo><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">Std</mi><mrow><mo>(</mo><mrow><msub><mrow><mrow><mo>{</mo><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="bold">Pk</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="bold-italic">rt</mi></mrow></msub><mrow><mo>(</mo><mrow><msubsup><mrow><mi>X</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>p</mi></mrow><mrow><mrow><mo>(</mo><mrow><mi>k</mi><mo>→</mo><msub><mrow><mi>k</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></mrow><mo>)</mo></mrow></mrow></msubsup></mrow><mo>)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo>}</mo></mrow></mrow><mrow><mi>k</mi><mo>∈</mo><msub><mrow><mi>N</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>p</mi></mrow></msub></mrow></msub></mrow><mo>)</mo></mrow><mo>,</mo><mspace width="0.25em"></mspace><msub><mrow><mi>D</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>r</mi><mi>t</mi></mrow></msub></mrow><mo>)</mo></mrow></math></span>. The alignment reliability score <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" id="IEq105"><math id="d33e4169"><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="bold-italic">AR</mi></mrow><mrow><mfenced close="}" open="{"><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>X</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>p</mi></mrow></msub></mrow></mfenced></mrow></msub></math></span> is 1 if the patch’s peak rt-location appears in a location consistent with the rest of the group, and 0 if the peak location is an outlier.</p></section><section id="Sec23"><h3 class="pmc_sec_title">Peak quality filtering</h3>
<p id="Par50">Stage-2 pins are used to learn the numerical thresholds for quality features. We define <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" id="IEq106"><math id="d33e4191"><mi>g</mi></math></span><span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" id="IEq107"><math id="d33e4197"><mrow><mo>(</mo><mrow><mi>X</mi></mrow><mo>)</mo></mrow></math></span> as a function that acts on an individual peak from a peak-group, <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" id="IEq108"><math id="d33e4208"><mi>X</mi><mo>∈</mo><msub><mrow><mrow><mo>{</mo><mrow><msubsup><mrow><mi>X</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>p</mi></mrow><mrow><mrow><mo>(</mo><mrow><mi>k</mi></mrow><mo>)</mo></mrow></mrow></msubsup></mrow><mo>}</mo></mrow></mrow><mrow><mi>k</mi><mo>∈</mo><msub><mrow><mi>N</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>p</mi></mrow></msub></mrow></msub></math></span>, and returns a quality score between -1 and 1. We define an individual-level score threshold, <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" id="IEq109"><math id="d33e4241"><msub><mrow><mi>t</mi><mi>h</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>g</mi></mrow></msub></math></span>, such that if <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" id="IEq110"><math id="d33e4253"><mi>g</mi></math></span><span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" id="IEq111"><math id="d33e4259"><mrow><mo>(</mo><mrow><mi>X</mi></mrow><mo>)</mo></mrow><mo>&lt;</mo><msub><mrow><mi>t</mi><mi>h</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>g</mi></mrow></msub></math></span>, then the peak is removed from the peak-group. We define a feature-level score threshold, <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" id="IEq112"><math id="d33e4278"><msub><mrow><mi>t</mi><mi>h</mi></mrow><mrow><mrow><mo>{</mo><mrow><mi>g</mi></mrow><mo>}</mo></mrow></mrow></msub></math></span>, such that if <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" id="IEq113"><math id="d33e4294"><msub><mrow><mi>Q</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>75</mn><mi>%</mi></mrow></msub><mrow><mo>(</mo><mrow><msub><mrow><mrow><mo>{</mo><mrow><mi>g</mi><mrow><mo>(</mo><mrow><msubsup><mrow><mi>X</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>p</mi></mrow><mrow><mrow><mo>(</mo><mrow><mi>k</mi></mrow><mo>)</mo></mrow></mrow></msubsup></mrow><mo>)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo>}</mo></mrow></mrow><mrow><mi>k</mi><mo>∈</mo><msub><mrow><mi>N</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>p</mi></mrow></msub></mrow></msub></mrow><mo>)</mo></mrow><mo>&lt;</mo><msub><mrow><mi>t</mi><mi>h</mi></mrow><mrow><mrow><mo>{</mo><mrow><mi>g</mi></mrow><mo>}</mo></mrow></mrow></msub></math></span>, then the full peak-group is removed from the list of possible features. We learn <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" id="IEq114"><math id="d33e4351"><msub><mrow><mi>t</mi><mi>h</mi></mrow><mrow><mrow><mo>{</mo><mrow><mi>g</mi></mrow><mo>}</mo></mrow></mrow></msub></math></span> from the set of pins, H, by taking the 5<sup>th</sup> percentile of the <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" id="IEq115"><math id="d33e4369"><mi>g</mi></math></span><span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" id="IEq116"><math id="d33e4375"><mrow><mo>(</mo><mrow><mi>X</mi></mrow><mo>)</mo></mrow></math></span> for all the individual peaks from the pin groups:</p>
<table class="disp-formula p" id="Equ9"><tr><td class="formula"><math id="d33e4387" display="block"><msub><mrow><mi>t</mi><mi>h</mi></mrow><mrow><mrow><mo>{</mo><mrow><mi>g</mi></mrow><mo>}</mo></mrow></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mrow><mi>Q</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>5</mn><mi>%</mi></mrow></msub><mfenced close=")" open="("><mrow><msub><mrow><mo mathsize="big">⋃</mo></mrow><mrow><mi>p</mi><mo>∈</mo><mi>H</mi></mrow></msub><msub><mrow><mfenced close="}" open="{"><mrow><mi>g</mi><mrow><mo>(</mo><mrow><msubsup><mrow><mi>X</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>p</mi></mrow><mrow><mfenced close=")" open="("><mrow><mi>k</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow></msubsup></mrow><mo>)</mo></mrow></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mi>k</mi><mo>∈</mo><msub><mrow><mi>N</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>p</mi></mrow></msub></mrow></msub></mrow></mfenced></math></td></tr></table>
<p id="Par51">Then, the individual-level thresholds are defined from the feature-level thresholds:</p>
<table class="disp-formula p" id="Equ10"><tr><td class="formula"><math id="d33e4447" display="block"><msub><mrow><mi>t</mi><mi>h</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>g</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mrow><mi>t</mi><mi>h</mi></mrow><mrow><mrow><mo>{</mo><mrow><mi>g</mi></mrow><mo>}</mo></mrow></mrow></msub><mo>/</mo><mn>3</mn></math></td></tr></table>
<p id="Par52">We use the above formula to define the minimum acceptable autocorrelation score for a peak-group and individual peak (<span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" id="IEq117"><math id="d33e4473"><mi>g</mi><mo>=</mo><mspace width="0.25em"></mspace><mi mathvariant="bold-italic">AC</mi></math></span><strong>)</strong>, and the minimum acceptable cross-correlation score for a peak-group and individual peak (<span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" id="IEq118"><math id="d33e4486"><mi>g</mi><mo>=</mo><mspace width="0.25em"></mspace><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="bold-italic">XC</mi></mrow><mrow><msup><mrow><mi>X</mi></mrow><mrow><mrow><mo>(</mo><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>k</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></mrow><mo>)</mo></mrow></mrow></msup></mrow></msub></math></span>), as well as the minimum acceptable alignment reliability score for an individual peak <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" id="IEq119"><math id="d33e4513"><mrow><mo>(</mo><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>g</mi><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="bold-italic">AR</mi></mrow><mrow><mfenced close="}" open="{"><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>X</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>p</mi></mrow></msub></mrow></mfenced><mo>,</mo><msub><mrow><mi>D</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>r</mi><mi>t</mi></mrow></msub></mrow></msub></mrow><mo>)</mo></mrow></math></span>.</p>
<section id="Sec24"><h4 class="pmc_sec_title">Missing peak search</h4>
<p id="Par53">After filtering, the original patches have passed through many different operations that used conservative filters to remove potential peaks and less-accurate drift maps to conduct the alignment. Therefore, the filtered peaks, while more trustworthy, have a high number of missing values due to the algorithm’s learning process. We can recover the missing peaks using the more accurate Stage-2 drift map.</p>
<p id="Par54">We consider a peak group with individual peaks found in <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" id="IEq120"><math id="d33e4550"><msub><mrow><mi>N</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>p</mi></mrow></msub></math></span> samples, <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" id="IEq121"><math id="d33e4561"><msub><mrow><mrow><mo>{</mo><mrow><msubsup><mrow><mi>X</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>p</mi></mrow><mrow><mrow><mo>(</mo><mrow><mi>k</mi></mrow><mo>)</mo></mrow></mrow></msubsup></mrow><mo>}</mo></mrow></mrow><mrow><mi>k</mi><mo>∈</mo><msub><mrow><mi>N</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>p</mi></mrow></msub></mrow></msub></math></span>, and choose a file, <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" id="IEq122"><math id="d33e4592"><mi>k</mi><mo>′</mo><mo>∉</mo><msub><mrow><mi>N</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>p</mi></mrow></msub></math></span>, that is not included in the peak group. We survey the individual peaks from the peak group, and for each surveyed peak, we estimate the position in file <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" id="IEq123"><math id="d33e4606"><mi>k</mi><mo>′</mo></math></span> where we would expect to find the corresponding peak. The bounding box estimates from all surveyed files are averaged together to create a final estimate of the expected peak location, <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" id="IEq124"><math id="d33e4614"><msubsup><mrow><mi>X</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>p</mi></mrow><mrow><mfenced close=")" open="("><mrow><mi>k</mi><mo>′</mo></mrow></mfenced></mrow></msubsup></math></span>, in sample <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" id="IEq125"><math id="d33e4631"><mi>k</mi><mo>′</mo></math></span>. The data in the expected peak location <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" id="IEq126"><math id="d33e4639"><msubsup><mrow><mi>X</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>p</mi></mrow><mrow><mrow><mo>(</mo><mrow><mi>k</mi><mo>′</mo></mrow><mo>)</mo></mrow></mrow></msubsup></math></span> is added to the peak group if it passes the individual peak quality thresholds defined above. After the missing peaks have been added to their respective peak groups, the merging, scissor, and filtering operations discussed previously are repeated.</p></section></section><section id="Sec25"><h3 class="pmc_sec_title">F. Estimating intensity drift and normalization</h3>
<p id="Par55">mzLearn is equipped with a normalization method that estimates signal intensity drift caused by extended run order and batch effects. The first step, pin normalization, removes sample-wide technical variations, such as those due to differences in the sample concentration or sample injection volume. Where no pool or QC samples are interspersed between the primary LC/MS samples in large-scale cohorts, mzLearn creates synthetic QC through an iterative process and normalizes the data in each iteration. Finally, it estimates the intensity drifts for the cases of missing values in pool or synthetic pool samples.</p>
<section id="Sec26"><h4 class="pmc_sec_title">Pin normalization</h4>
<p id="Par56">Pin normalization corrects the sample-wide technical variations in the data. Peaks are normalized sample-by-sample by dividing by the total intensity of the stage-3 pins. Stage-3 pins are defined as those features found in ≥60% of the sample files with auto-correlation and cross-correlation scores above the 10th percentile.</p></section><section id="Sec27"><h4 class="pmc_sec_title">Creating synthetic pool samples</h4>
<p id="Par57">mzLearn creates Synthetic QC samples from the primary samples that approximate experimental QC samples, which can be used to estimate the feature-specific intensity drift when no QC samples are available in the public domain datasets. First, primary samples are clustered based on a low-dimensional representation of their current feature intensity profile, with each cluster constrained to include only consecutive samples in the run order. The number of clusters is chosen to maximize the Silhouette Coefficient while maintaining at least 20 samples in each cluster. Second, Synthetic pools are created by averaging the feature intensity of all samples within a cluster. Then, peaks are partially normalized to the intensity of their corresponding synthetic pool, where the silhouette coefficient of the clustering modulates the normalization factor. This clustering, synthetic QC generation, and modulated normalization process iterates until run-order consecutive clusters have a consistent near-zero silhouette coefficient.</p></section><section id="Sec28"><h4 class="pmc_sec_title">Feature-specific intensity drift-map</h4>
<p id="Par58">QC samples (or their synthetic equivalents) can be used to estimate the intensity drift for a specific feature. In the case when a feature, <em>f</em>, has no missing values in the QC samples, mzLearn computes the normalization of its intensity in sample file k, <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" id="IEq127"><math id="d33e4676"><msubsup><mrow><mi>u</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>f</mi></mrow><mrow><mrow><mo>(</mo><mrow><mi>k</mi></mrow><mo>)</mo></mrow></mrow></msubsup></math></span>, as:</p>
<table class="disp-formula p" id="Equ11"><tr><td class="formula"><math id="d33e4693" display="block"><mi>N</mi><mi>o</mi><mi>r</mi><mi>m</mi><mrow><mo>(</mo><mrow><msubsup><mrow><mi>u</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>f</mi></mrow><mrow><mrow><mo>(</mo><mrow><mi>k</mi></mrow><mo>)</mo></mrow></mrow></msubsup></mrow><mo>)</mo></mrow><mo>=</mo><msubsup><mrow><mi>u</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>f</mi></mrow><mrow><mrow><mo>(</mo><mrow><mi>k</mi></mrow><mo>)</mo></mrow></mrow></msubsup><mo>/</mo><mspace width="0.25em"></mspace><mfrac><mrow><msubsup><mrow><mo mathsize="big">∑</mo></mrow><mrow><mi>i</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></msubsup><msubsup><mrow><mi>u</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>f</mi></mrow><mrow><mrow><mo>(</mo><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>q</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>k</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>i</mi></mrow></msub></mrow><mo>)</mo></mrow></mrow></msubsup><mo>∣</mo><msub><mrow><mi>t</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>k</mi></mrow></msub><mo>−</mo><msub><mrow><mi>t</mi></mrow><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>q</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>k</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>i</mi></mrow></msub></mrow></msub><mo>∣</mo></mrow><mrow><msubsup><mrow><mo>∑</mo></mrow><mrow><mi>i</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></msubsup><mo>∣</mo><msub><mrow><mi>t</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>k</mi></mrow></msub><mo>−</mo><msub><mrow><mi>t</mi></mrow><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>q</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>k</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>i</mi></mrow></msub></mrow></msub><mo>∣</mo></mrow></mfrac><mo>,</mo></math></td></tr></table>
<p>where <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" id="IEq128"><math id="d33e4806"><msub><mrow><mi>t</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>k</mi></mrow></msub></math></span> is the experiment run time associated with sample k, <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" id="IEq129"><math id="d33e4817"><msub><mrow><mi>t</mi></mrow><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>q</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>k</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>i</mi></mrow></msub></mrow></msub></math></span> is the experiment run time associated with pool sample <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" id="IEq130"><math id="d33e4835"><msub><mrow><mi>q</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>k</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>i</mi></mrow></msub></math></span>, and <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" id="IEq131"><math id="d33e4848"><msub><mrow><mi>q</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>k</mi><mo>,</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub><mo>,</mo><msub><mrow><mi>q</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>k</mi><mo>,</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mo>,</mo><msub><mrow><mi>q</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>k</mi><mo>,</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msub><mo>,</mo><msub><mrow><mi>q</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>k</mi><mo>,</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow></msub></math></span> are the four pool samples closest to sample file k in the experiment run time. When <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" id="IEq132"><math id="d33e4885"><msubsup><mrow><mi>u</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>f</mi></mrow><mrow><mrow><mo>(</mo><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>q</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>k</mi></mrow></msub></mrow><mo>)</mo></mrow></mrow></msubsup></math></span> is a missing value; mzLearn estimates the value of <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" id="IEq133"><math id="d33e4906"><msubsup><mrow><mi>u</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>f</mi></mrow><mrow><mrow><mo>(</mo><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>q</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>k</mi></mrow></msub></mrow><mo>)</mo></mrow></mrow></msubsup></math></span> using nearby existing features in the QC sample.</p>
<p id="Par59">mzLearn can correct feature-specific intensity drift, even when features have missing values. mzLearn considers the set of features with ≥60% sample frequency in the set of QC or synthetic QC pins, <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" id="IEq134"><math id="d33e4929"><msub><mrow><mi>H</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>p</mi><mi>o</mi><mi>o</mi><mi>l</mi></mrow></msub></math></span>. For each QC or synthetic QC sample, we compare the intensity of the identified features with the QC- or synthetic QC-wide mean <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" id="IEq135"><math id="d33e4943"><msub><mrow><mi>μ</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>f</mi></mrow></msub></math></span> to create an intensity drift map for that QC or synthetic QC sample. This intensity drift map, <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" id="IEq136"><math id="d33e4954"><msubsup><mrow><mi>d</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>u</mi></mrow><mrow><mrow><mo>(</mo><mrow><mi>q</mi></mrow><mo>)</mo></mrow></mrow></msubsup><mrow><mo>(</mo><mrow><mi>r</mi><mi>t</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>m</mi><mo>/</mo><mi>z</mi></mrow><mo>)</mo></mrow></math></span>, is used to estimate the intensity for the missing feature, <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" id="IEq137"><math id="d33e4981"><msubsup><mrow><mi>u</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>f</mi></mrow><mrow><mrow><mo>(</mo><mrow><mi>q</mi></mrow><mo>)</mo></mrow></mrow></msubsup></math></span> using its <em>rt</em>-<em>m/z</em> coordinates:</p>
<table class="disp-formula p" id="Equ12"><tr><td class="formula"><math id="d33e5005" display="block"><msubsup><mrow><mi>u</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>f</mi></mrow><mrow><mrow><mo>(</mo><mrow><mi>q</mi></mrow><mo>)</mo></mrow></mrow></msubsup><mspace width="0.25em"></mspace><mo>=</mo><mspace width="0.25em"></mspace><msub><mrow><mi>μ</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>f</mi></mrow></msub><msubsup><mrow><mspace width="0.25em"></mspace><mi>d</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>u</mi></mrow><mrow><mrow><mo>(</mo><mrow><mi>q</mi></mrow><mo>)</mo></mrow></mrow></msubsup><mrow><mo>(</mo><mrow><msubsup><mrow><mi>r</mi><mi>t</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>f</mi></mrow><mrow><mrow><mo>(</mo><mrow><mi>q</mi></mrow><mo>)</mo></mrow></mrow></msubsup><mo>,</mo><msubsup><mrow><mi>m</mi><mi>z</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>f</mi></mrow><mrow><mrow><mo>(</mo><mrow><mi>q</mi></mrow><mo>)</mo></mrow></mrow></msubsup></mrow><mo>)</mo></mrow><mo>,</mo></math></td></tr></table>
<p>where <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" id="IEq138"><math id="d33e5072"><msubsup><mrow><mi>r</mi><mi>t</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>f</mi></mrow><mrow><mrow><mo>(</mo><mrow><mi>q</mi></mrow><mo>)</mo></mrow></mrow></msubsup></math></span> and <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" id="IEq139"><math id="d33e5090"><msubsup><mrow><mi>m</mi><mi>z</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>f</mi></mrow><mrow><mrow><mo>(</mo><mrow><mi>q</mi></mrow><mo>)</mo></mrow></mrow></msubsup></math></span> are the <em>rt</em> and <em>m/z</em> coordinates of the missing feature in the <em>q</em><sup>th</sup> QC or synthetic QC sample. The drift map <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" id="IEq140"><math id="d33e5119"><msubsup><mrow><mi>d</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>u</mi></mrow><mrow><mrow><mo>(</mo><mrow><mi>q</mi></mrow><mo>)</mo></mrow></mrow></msubsup><mrow><mo>(</mo><mrow><mi>r</mi><mi>t</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>m</mi><mo>/</mo><mi>z</mi></mrow><mo>)</mo></mrow></math></span> is created by interpolation using the existing (not-missing) features <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" id="IEq141"><math id="d33e5146"><mi>h</mi><mi>ϵ</mi><msub><mrow><mi>H</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>p</mi><mi>o</mi><mi>o</mi><mi>l</mi></mrow></msub></math></span> in the <em>q</em><sup>th</sup> QC or synthetic QC sample, <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" id="IEq142"><math id="d33e5166"><msubsup><mrow><mi>d</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>u</mi></mrow><mrow><mrow><mo>(</mo><mrow><mi>q</mi></mrow><mo>)</mo></mrow></mrow></msubsup><mrow><mo>(</mo><mrow><msubsup><mrow><mi>r</mi><mi>t</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>h</mi></mrow><mrow><mrow><mo>(</mo><mrow><mi>q</mi></mrow><mo>)</mo></mrow></mrow></msubsup><mo>,</mo><msubsup><mrow><mi>m</mi><mi>z</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>h</mi></mrow><mrow><mrow><mo>(</mo><mrow><mi>q</mi></mrow><mo>)</mo></mrow></mrow></msubsup></mrow><mo>)</mo></mrow><mspace width="0.25em"></mspace><mo>=</mo><mspace width="0.25em"></mspace><msubsup><mrow><mi>u</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>h</mi></mrow><mrow><mrow><mo>(</mo><mrow><mi>q</mi></mrow><mo>)</mo></mrow></mrow></msubsup><mspace width="0.25em"></mspace><mo>/</mo><msub><mrow><mi>μ</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>h</mi></mrow></msub></math></span>.</p></section></section><section id="Sec29"><h3 class="pmc_sec_title">G. Data-driven threshold learning and update schedule</h3>
<section id="Sec30"><h4 class="pmc_sec_title"><em>initialization (Stage 0)</em></h4>
<p id="Par60">We apply a one-time seed of <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" id="IEq143"><math id="d33e5241"><msubsup><mrow><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>A</mi><mi>C</mi></mrow><mrow><mrow><mo>(</mo><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow><mo>)</mo></mrow></mrow></msubsup><mo>=</mo><mn>0.5</mn></math></span>, to filter obviously noisy patches and <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" id="IEq144"><math id="d33e5261"><msubsup><mrow><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>X</mi><mi>C</mi></mrow><mrow><mrow><mo>(</mo><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow><mo>)</mo></mrow></mrow></msubsup><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></span>, to retain non-negative cross-correlations.</p></section><section id="Sec31"><h4 class="pmc_sec_title">Stage-1 <em>pins and drift map</em>
</h4>
<p id="Par61">We define pins as correlated patch groups present in all samples whose group-average cross-correlation ≥0.8 and whose average autocorrelation exceeds the cohort average. The 0.8 coherence anchor yields high-precision alignment landmarks; percentile bounds make the search window data-adaptive and robust to outliers. Pins generate a non-linear rt-drift map by weighted averaging of local pin drifts (weights decrease with rt–m/z distance). The lower/upper bounds of expected drift are the weighted 1st/99th percentiles and restrict the search window for cross-correlation in later stages.</p></section><section id="Sec32"><h4 class="pmc_sec_title"><em>Learning thresholds from pins (Stages 2–3)</em></h4>
<p id="Par62">For each quality metric g∈{AC, XC, AR}, we compute the empirical distribution of per-peak scores across the current pin set and set the feature-level cutoff to the 5th percentile. A 5th-percentile quantile policy is scale-free and controls tail noise while preserving recall; three passes are sufficient to reach a stable feature set and drift map. A candidate feature passes if its group-level score if it is more than the cutoff; a member peak is retained if its individual score is higher than the same cutoff. Thresholds are relearned after each re-alignment; mzLearn converges in three iterations.</p></section><section id="Sec33"><h4 class="pmc_sec_title"><em>Normalization sets and synthetic-QC stopping</em></h4>
<p id="Par63">For pin normalization, we define Stage-3 pins as features found in ≥60% of samples whose autocorrelation and cross-correlation exceed the cohort 10th percentile. Synthetic-QC clustering is constrained to run-order-contiguous groups (≥20 samples), iterated until the silhouette coefficient approaches 0 (no residual run-order structure). These thresholds are conservative—high enough to avoid noisy anchors, low enough to ensure sufficient coverage for normalization and drift control.</p>
<p id="Par64">The sensitivity analysis by varying the learned quantiles and the iteration count, the detected feature sets and alignment residuals changed minimally, and downstream conclusions were unchanged. Each run writes a machine-readable CSV (params_overall.csv) with instrument precision, <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" id="IEq145"><math id="d33e5302"><mi>M</mi><msub><mrow><mi>Z</mi></mrow><mrow><mstyle><mtext>coarse</mtext></mstyle></mrow></msub></math></span>, <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" id="IEq146"><math id="d33e5315"><mi>R</mi><msub><mrow><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><mstyle><mtext>coarse</mtext></mstyle></mrow></msub></math></span>, and global <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" id="IEq147"><math id="d33e5328"><mi>m</mi><mo>/</mo><mi>z</mi></math></span>–RT bounds/dispersion used to initialize patching and alignment; this file is deposited alongside results to document the learned and initialized settings at run time.</p></section></section><section id="Sec34"><h3 class="pmc_sec_title">mzLearn implementation and runtime</h3>
<p id="Par65">mzLearn is implemented in Python. The detector/alignment engine uses the scientific stack (NumPy/SciPy/pandas) for correlation-based operations and ingests centroided MS¹ from mzML via a Python parser. The software environment is provided as a version-pinned Docker image with a matching requirements.txt for reproducibility. Runtime is dominated by cross-sample correlation and iterative drift-aware re-alignment and therefore increases with the number of mzML files in a cohort; the memory footprint remains modest due to tiling and batched processing. For larger studies, we recommend multi-core parallelism and, when available, multi-node execution.</p></section><section id="Sec35"><h3 class="pmc_sec_title">Evaluation datasets and sources</h3>
<p id="Par66">We evaluated mzLearn on publicly available LC–MS datasets. Each dataset is referenced in text by repository and accession Supplementary Table <a href="#MOESM2" class="usa-link">2</a>, including MassIVE: MSV000086486⁵¹, MSV000087434<sup><a href="#CR73"
